--- Page 238 ---
<meta>This page continues the Elector of Saxony’s response. The catchword from the previous page "alle-" combines with the first word "gnüge" to mean "sufficiently" or "thoroughly." This section focuses on the Elector's defense of his loyalty and his appeal to the constitutional laws of the Holy Roman Empire.</meta>

sufficiently learned / and have not only carried such [loyalty] in our mouths / but have also proven it so gloriously and publicly in our works—with a faithful German heart and useful deeds **exemplarily** <note>original: *exemplariter* — serving as a model or example</note> / that we have justly earned high praise and a better thanks for it than is now, unfortunately, given to us in return for our services rendered so laboriously and bravely in manifold ways. On the other hand, it is also manifest what His **Imperial Majesty** <note>original: *Keyserliche Maystet* — referring to the Holy Roman Emperor, Ferdinand II</note> / when he was raised to the Imperial Highness through **God's** <note>original: *GOTT*</note> special providence and the unanimous free election of all the **Lord Electors** <note>original: *Churfürsten* — the elite group of princes who chose the Emperor</note> / and received the crown and **scepter** <note>original: *Scepter*</note> / solemnly promised to us and other Electors and Estates. We do once again firmly reassure ourselves of this / and take comfort in the promised Imperial protection. 

But that we are now nevertheless pressured with such force / and met with such a reward instead of the promised thanks and **repayment** <note>original: *remuneratio*</note> / we commend to Almighty God, who judges rightly. We must indeed confess / that such **predictions** <note>original: *prognosticen* — warnings or forecasts of political trouble</note> were presented to us long ago / but as an upright German Elector, we could not believe such things / though we now experience them as a sorrowful reality.

And because we cannot further explain ourselves—without violating our honor, status, freedom, and name—other than that we wish to most willingly and submissively perform for His Imperial Majesty, in constant and unfeigned loyalty, everything that the beneficial and highly-sworn **Laws of the Empire** <note>original: *Reichs-Geseze*</note> / by which the **Head and Members** <note>An early modern political metaphor: the Emperor is the "Head" and the Imperial Estates (princes, cities, etc.) are the "Members" of the body politic</note> are bound, require / therefore, we remain in the most humble hope toward our dear Emperor that His Imperial Majesty will / as a just and mild regent / not press us further / nor demand more of us / and least of all allow us to be persecuted and besieged with such cruel and unheard-of force on that account. Moreover, this is such a matter which our fellow Lord Electors—who, by virtue of the **Golden Bull** <note>original: *Güldenen Bull* — the fundamental "constitution" of the Holy Roman Empire issued in 1356</note>, are duty-bound to faithfully guard the freedom of the Empire as much as we are, according to their office as Electors and the heavy duties they have performed—will certainly, out of heroic bravery and excellent wisdom, no more than we [will]

<summary>The Elector of Saxony defends his record of loyal service to the Emperor and argues that the current military aggression against him violates the Emperor's coronation oaths and the fundamental laws of the Empire, such as the Golden Bull.</summary>
<keywords>Elector of Saxony, Holy Roman Emperor, Golden Bull, Imperial Law, 30 Years War, loyalty, Electors, constitutional rights</keywords>

--- Page 239 ---
<meta>This page continues the Elector of Saxony’s formal response to Imperial pressure. Having previously asserted his historical loyalty, he now addresses specific demands for compliance with Imperial edicts and defends his delay in responding, citing the ongoing devastation of his lands by military forces.</meta>

...expressed. Therefore, by citing various motives, you seek once more to ensure that we do not enter into any **desperate plans** <note>original: *desperata Consilia* — risky or extreme political measures taken in times of crisis</note>, but rather that we should, through most obedient **compliance** <note>original: *parition* — formal obedience to a legal order</note>, set to work without refusal what the **Imperial Mandates of Avocation** <note>original: *Keyserliche Avocatorial-Mandata* — legal decrees issued by the Emperor to recall subjects from foreign service or to stop illegal activities</note> contained, and what was most graciously intended through the **Instruction** <note>original: *Instruction* — official orders or guidelines</note> handed to us by your sub-delegates. For in the unexpected and contrary case that **compliance** did not follow, you would necessarily have to proceed against us and put into effect what was in accordance with the command of his **Imperial Majesty** <note>original: *Keyserliche Maystät* — the Holy Roman Emperor</note>, our most gracious Lord, and what unavoidable necessity and the **nature of the war** <note>original: *Kriegsbeschaffenheit*</note> would unalterably require.

Now, at the outset, there was no need for the thanks offered; for our duty toward the aforementioned **Imperial Majesty** required such a thing. We would have also long ago answered your above-mentioned letter regarding the main points, had we not remained in the constant and good confidence that the robbing, plundering, and burning in our lands would have ceased—since you report that you have forbidden such things—and also that those places taken by force would have been fully **restored** <note>original: *restituiret*</note>. Therefore, you will not view this delay as unjustified.

Regarding the main matter, we hope that in the written **decision** <note>original: *resolution*</note> granted to the Lords Sub-delegates, we have declared ourselves in such a most humble and commendable manner as our obligated obedience, the **Imperial Constitutions** <note>original: *Reichs-Constitutiones* — the fundamental laws of the Holy Roman Empire</note>, tradition, and the current state of affairs demand. We also lived in the good hope that His Imperial Majesty would require nothing more from us than what we owe to the sworn **Laws of the Empire**, and would therefore have been most graciously satisfied with our most obedient offer. Finally, we must leave the **judgment** <note>original: *judication*</note> in this matter to those at home and abroad, indeed to the whole honorable world.

What kind of obedience we are indebted to perform for the Roman Imperial Majesty, as a loyal **Prince-Elector** <note>original: *Churfürst* — one of the high-ranking princes who elected the Emperor</note> of the Empire, is—praise be to God—known to us; and we have, in our Electoral government conducted for over twenty years now through God's goodness...

<summary>The Elector of Saxony defends his delay in complying with Imperial mandates, pointing to the ongoing plundering of his territory despite official prohibitions. He asserts that his loyalty is governed by the Imperial Constitution and asks the world to judge his twenty-year record of faithful service.</summary>
<keywords>Elector of Saxony, Imperial Mandates, Thirty Years' War, Imperial Constitution, compliance, restoration of territory, John George I, political loyalty</keywords>

--- Page 240 ---
<meta>This page continues the Elector of Saxony’s formal protestation. He transitions from defending his past actions to expressing a sense of betrayal, arguing that his loyalty to the Emperor should have earned him protection rather than the military aggression he is currently facing. He concludes by appealing to the constitutional protections of the Holy Roman Empire.</meta>

...sufficiently learned, and have not merely spoken of it with our mouths, but have also demonstrated it so world-famously in our works through a faithful German heart and useful deeds, as an example, that we have justly earned high praise and a better gratitude for the brave services we have rendered in so many ways. On the other hand, it is also manifest what His **Imperial Majesty** <note>original: *Keys. Mayst.* — Holy Roman Emperor Ferdinand II</note>—when he was raised to Imperial Highness through **God’s** <note>original: *GOTTES*</note> singular providence and the unanimous free election of all the Lords **Electors** <note>original: *Churfürsten* — the high-ranking princes who elected the Emperor</note>, and received the crown and **scepter** <note>original: *Scepter*</note>—most solemnly promised to us and other Electors and Estates. We still firmly rely on this and take comfort in the promised Imperial protection.

However, that we are nevertheless now pressured with such force, and encounter such a "reward" instead of the promised thanks and **repayment** <note>original: *remuneratiō* — Latin for compensation or reward</note>, we entrust to God the Almighty, who judges rightly. We must indeed confess that such a **prediction** <note>original: *prognosticon* — a forecast or omen of future events</note> was presented to us long ago; but as an upright **German** <note>original: *Teutscher*</note> Elector, we could not believe such a thing—yet we now experience it in this present, sorrowful reality.

And because we cannot further explain ourselves—without violating our honor, status, liberty, and name—other than stating that we wish to most willingly and submissively render to His Imperial Majesty, in constant and unfeigned loyalty, everything that the beneficial and solemnly sworn **Imperial Laws** <note>original: *Reichs-Gesetze* — the legal framework of the Holy Roman Empire</note> (to which the head and the members <note>A traditional metaphor where the Emperor is the "head" and the Estates are the "limbs" of the body politic.</note> are bound) require. Therefore, we remain once again in the most submissive hope toward our dear Emperor that His Imperial Majesty, as a just and mild regent, will not press us further nor demand more of us—least of all allow us to be persecuted and oppressed with such cruel and unheard-of violence for this reason. Furthermore, this is such a matter which our fellow Electors—who, by virtue of the **Golden Bull** <note>original: *Güldene Bull* — the 1356 decree defining the constitutional structure of the Empire</note> and the liberty of the Empire, are just as duty-bound as we are to faithfully safeguard it according to their eminent electoral office and the heavy oaths they have sworn—and certainly, out of heroic bravery and excellent wisdom, they will no more than we...

<summary>The Elector of Saxony expresses his deep disillusionment, claiming that despite his proven loyalty, the Emperor has met his service with military force rather than the promised protection. He appeals to the Imperial Laws and the Golden Bull, hoping his fellow Electors will recognize this breach of constitutional duty.</summary>
<keywords>Elector of Saxony, Holy Roman Emperor, Golden Bull, Imperial Laws, loyalty, betrayal, Ferdinand II, constitutional rights, German liberty</keywords>

--- Page 241 ---
<meta>This page continues the formal protestation of John George I, Elector of Saxony, dated September 1631. In the preceding text, the Elector argued that his loyalty to the Emperor should have guaranteed his safety. Here, he concludes his argument by listing the atrocities committed against his lands and people, which have forced him to take defensive action despite his desire for peace.</meta>

...to be set aside or affected; and We, therefore, according to the contents of the so dearly sworn **Electoral Union** <term>original: *Churfürstlichen Verein* — a defensive alliance of the Protestant Electors within the Holy Roman Empire</term>, are not in the least empowered to yield anything to them.

And despite this, they have nevertheless proceeded incessantly in Our lands with robbing, plundering, violating women, burning, and other cruelties, one way after another, and have occupied various locations and places therein by force. Just as in these recent days, beyond previous manifold plunderings, has happened to Our cities and administrative districts of **Freyburg** <note>modern: Freyburg (Unstrut)</note>, **Micheln** <note>modern: Mücheln</note> (both of which were also set on fire and burned), **Weißenfels**, **Pegau**, and **Zeitz**. In such places, they have dealt very evilly with Our Chancellor and government councilors, as well as other officials, some of whom were shaken by the head with ropes and had their thumbs crushed with screws <note>a reference to the *Daumenschraube* or thumbscrew, a common instrument of torture during the Thirty Years' War</note>. They have also committed public violation of women and many other gross excesses, behaving worse than barbarians; and furthermore, they have explicitly declared that they intend to do the same to Our other cities, districts, and subjects. Since We are most movingly called upon by Our subjects with many sighs and tears for protection and rescue from such tribulations, We are pressed and forced to consider such means by which to turn away such insult and violation, and to take such a resolution as the law, nature, and tradition teach Us and place in Our hands—the likes of which We would otherwise never have considered.

We **protest** <note>original: *Contestiren* — a formal legal and diplomatic declaration of witness</note> however once more before **God** and all the world, that We are excused in this matter, and yet no less desire to remain at all times in Our due loyalty, love, and obedience to His **Imperial Majesty** and the Empire, unchanged. We are from the heart desirous of an honorable, secure, and lasting **universal peace** <note>original: *universal-Friedens* — a peace encompassing the entire Holy Roman Empire</note>; We also urge you once more herewith that you should faithfully help to promote the same in your own place. We wished to report this to you in reply, and remain favorably inclined toward you with Electoral grace. Given at **Torgau** on the third of September, in the year 1631.

-> E N D E. <-

<summary>The Elector of Saxony details the brutal pillaging and torture inflicted on his cities by Imperial forces, declaring that these atrocities have forced him to take defensive measures. He concludes by reaffirming his loyalty to the Emperor while calling for a general peace.</summary>
<keywords>Thirty Years' War, Elector of Saxony, Torgau, war crimes, torture, thumbscrews, Weißenfels, Zeitz, universal peace, 1631</keywords>

<meta>This page marks a shift in the collection from the personal protestations of the Elector of Saxony (1631) to the formal public declaration issued by King Gustavus Adolphus of Sweden upon his intervention in the Holy Roman Empire in 1630. This title page serves as the official introduction to the Swedish King's legal and moral justification for war.</meta>

# Causes

->Why<-
->The Most Serene / Most Mighty<-
->Prince and Lord / Lord<-

# GUSTAV ADOLF.

->King of the Swedes, Goths, and Vandals <note>original: *Schweden, Gothen vnd Wenden* — These were the traditional titles of the Swedish monarchs, claiming ancient lineage from the tribes that once overran the Roman Empire.</note>,<-
->Grand Prince in Finland / Duke of<-
->Estonia <term>original: *Ehesten* — refers to the Duchy of Estonia, a dominion of the Swedish Empire</term> and Karelia / Lord of<-
->Ingria <term>original: *Ingermanlandt* — a historical region located along the shore of the Gulf of Finland, vital for controlling Baltic trade</term>,<-

->Has at long last<-
->Been compelled to set foot upon German soil with military force.<-
